          // Time to maturity\n    double vol;         // Volatility\n    double value;       // Expected value (for validation)\n    double tol;         // Tolerance\n};\n\n// Standard normal cumulative distribution function\ninline double cumulativeNormal(const double x) noexcept {\n    return 0.5 * (1.0 + std::erf(x * M_SQRT1_2));\n}\n\n// Standard normal probability density function\ninline double normalPDF(const double x) noexcept {\n    return M_1_SQRTPI * M_SQRT1_2 * exp(-0.5 * x * x);\n}\n\n// Black-Scholes formula for European options\ndouble blackScholes(const OptionInput& option) noexcept {\n    const double S = option.spot;\n    const double K = option.strike;\n    const double r = option.r;\n    const double q = option.q;\n    const double T = option.t;\n    const double sigma = option.vol;\n    \n    if (T <= 0.0 || sigma <= 0.0) {\n        return 0.0;\n    }\n    \n    const double sigmaSqrtT = sigma * sqrt(T);\n    const double d1 = (log(S / K) + (r - q + 0.5 * sigma * sigma) * T) / sigmaSqrtT;\n    const double d2 = d1 - sigmaSqrtT;\n\n    const double discount = exp(-r * T);\n    const double spotDiscount = S * exp(-q * T);\n\n    // Only the two cumulative normals actually used by the selected branch are\n    // evaluated; {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CecvDkXRMBatkSdmdbHJH","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"All checks pass. Here's what I changed.\n\n**`black-scholes/black_scholes.cpp`**\n- **Parallel pricing loop** — `#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)` over the option array. Each option is independent, so results are bitwise identical to the serial version regardless of thread count.\n- **NUMA-aware data placement** — added a `DefaultInitAllocator` (leaves trivial elements uninitialized) so the `options` and `results` vectors aren't first-touched by the master thread. Both are then filled by parallel loops using the same `schedule(static)` mapping as the pricing loop, so every thread's pages land on its local NUMA node. This alone was worth ~1.5x on this 2-socket machine.\n- **Thread pinning** — `bindThreads()` pins each OpenMP thread to a distinct CPU from the process affinity mask (`sched_getaffinity` + `pthread_setaffinity_np`, both glibc). It is skipped when the user already set `OMP_PROC_BIND`/`OMP_PLACES`, in which case it just warms the thread pool. Without this, unbound threads migrate away from their first-touched memory (measured 41–52 ms vs 28 ms).\n- **Kernel restructuring** — hoisted `sigma*sqrt(T)` and `S*exp(-q*T)` into named values and moved the `cumulativeNormal` calls into their branches. Previously puts evaluated four `erf`s instead of two. Every expression is computed from the same operands in the same order, so this is value-preserving, which I verified bit-for-bit.\n- Added an `OpenMP threads: N` line to the existing startup output.\n\n**`black-scholes/CMakeLists.txt`** — `find_package(OpenMP REQUIRED COMPONENTS CXX)` and link `OpenMP::OpenMP_CXX`. Executable name unchanged, no new dependencies.\n\n**Correctness:** the `-r` hash is identical to the unmodified serial program at n = 10 000 / 1 000 003 / 50 000 000, and identical across 1, 3, 7, 64, 128, 200, 256 threads. `-v`, `-h`, `-n 0`, and the unknown-option path behave as before. Clean build under `-Wall -Wextra`.\n\n**Performance** (dual EPYC 7763, 128 cores / 256 threads, n = 50M):\n\n| config | time | speedup |\n|---|---|---|\n| original serial | 4364 ms | 1.0x |\n| OpenMP, default env | 28.2 ms | 155x |\n\nOne note on scope: I did not vectorize the kernel with `#pragma omp simd`. GCC would route `erf`/`exp`/`log` through libmvec, whose vector variants are not bit-identical to the scalar ones, which would change the validation hash. Given that the benchmark hashes results exactly, I kept the scalar math. Here's what I changed.\n\n**`black-scholes/black_scholes.cpp`**\n- **Parallel pricing loop** — `#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)` over the option array. Each option is independent, so results are bitwise identical to the serial version regardless of thread count.\n- **NUMA-aware data placement** — added a `DefaultInitAllocator` (leaves trivial elements uninitialized) so the `options` and `results` vectors aren't first-touched by the master thread. Both are then filled by parallel loops using the same `schedule(static)` mapping as the pricing loop, so every thread's pages land on its local NUMA node. This alone was worth ~1.5x on this 2-socket machine.\n- **Thread pinning** — `bindThreads()` pins each OpenMP thread to a distinct CPU from the process affinity mask (`sched_getaffinity` + `pthread_setaffinity_np`, both glibc). It is skipped when the user already set `OMP_PROC_BIND`/`OMP_PLACES`, in which case it just warms the thread pool. Without this, unbound threads migrate away from their first-touched memory (measured 41–52 ms vs 28 ms).\n- **Kernel restructuring** — hoisted `sigma*sqrt(T)` and `S*exp(-q*T)` into named values and moved the `cumulativeNormal` calls into their branches. Previously puts evaluated four `erf`s instead of two. Every expression is computed from the same operands in the same order, so this is value-preserving, which I verified bit-for-bit.\n- Added an `OpenMP threads: N` line to the existing startup output.\n\n**`black-scholes/CMakeLists.txt`** — `find_package(OpenMP REQUIRED COMPONENTS CXX)` and link `OpenMP::OpenMP_CXX`. Executable name unchanged, no new dependencies.\n\n**Correctness:** the `-r` hash is identical to the unmodified serial program at n = 10 000 / 1 000 003 / 50 000 000, and identical across 1, 3, 7, 64, 128, 200, 256 threads. `-v`, `-h`, `-n 0`, and the unknown-option path behave as before. Clean build under `-Wall -Wextra`.\n\n**Performance** (dual EPYC 7763, 128 cores / 256 threads, n = 50M):\n\n| config | time | speedup |\n|---|---|---|\n| original serial | 4364 ms | 1.0x |\n| OpenMP, default env | 28.2 ms | 155x |\n\nOne note on scope: I did not vectorize the kernel with `#pragma omp simd`. GCC would route `erf`/`exp`/`log` through libmvec, whose vector variants are not bit-identical to the scalar ones, which would change the validation hash. Given that the benchmark hashes results exactly, I kept the scalar math. If you'd rather trade exactness for more throughput, that's the next lever available.","ttft_ms":1770,"ttft_stream_ms":1432,"time_to_request_ms":66,"type":"result","duration_ms":417565,"uuid":"d986f536-2c09-4007-acd3-df14885e8732","queued_turn_count":0}
